Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I guess I’m here because things have felt… really out of proportion with me lately. I just moved into a new apartment, and at first I could barely get myself settled — like boxes everywhere, half-unpacked kitchen stuff, that kind of thing — and then all of a sudden I sort of flipped into this mode where I was saying yes to everything.

I agreed to do mentoring sessions, even though I hadn’t really planned for that. I took on extra client prototype work. And then I started completely redesigning my portfolio, like not just updating a few pieces, but acting like I needed to overhaul the whole thing immediately. It felt like I had to fix my entire life in one sprint, if that makes sense. Like everything needed to be optimized and rebuilt right now, and I couldn’t just leave things alone.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I did. Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling almost electric, like everything was suddenly possible and I was weirdly upbeat about it. Not just “in a good mood,” more like I had this charge under my skin and I could see all these fixes and improvements everywhere.

But it wasn’t only happy, exactly. It could switch really fast. One minute I’d be excited, talking too fast about some portfolio idea or how the apartment was finally going to come together, and then if something slowed me down even a little, I’d get so irritated. Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. In the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way of this whole plan I had to get everything moving.

Then afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? It was way bigger than the situation called for.

Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. It’s hard to describe because it wasn’t just “I had a lot of energy.” It felt almost electric, like I was plugged into something, and I’d be really upbeat and convinced I could handle everything all at once.

So yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.

Yeah… yes. I have. It’s been happening lately in a way that feels really physical, not just like I’m having a bad day or I’m stressed.

I’ll wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.

It’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.

Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It didn’t feel like I was depressed right then. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.

But it would flip really fast into irritation. Like if something slowed me down even slightly, I’d react as if it was a much bigger deal than it actually was. I snapped at the contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too. In the moment it honestly felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way on purpose, even though now I can see that’s not fair.

So yes, I’d say there have been times when the energetic and irritable feeling was its own thing, not just me being sad before or after.

I took on extra client prototype work. And then I started completely redesigning my portfolio, like not just updating a few pieces, but acting like I needed to overhaul the whole thing immediately. It felt like I had to fix my entire life in one sprint, if that makes sense. Like everything needed to be optimized and rebuilt right now, and I couldn’t just leave things alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or energetic during this period of taking on extra work and redesigning your portfolio?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling almost electric, like everything was suddenly possible and I was weirdly upbeat about it. Not just “in a good mood,” more like I had this charge under my skin and I could see all these fixes and improvements everywhere.\n\nBut it wasn’t only happy, exactly. It could switch really fast. One minute I’d be excited, talking too fast about some portfolio idea or how the apartment was finally going to come together, and then if something slowed me down even a little, I’d get so irritated. Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. In the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way of this whole plan I had to get everything moving.\n\nThen afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. They’ve been kind of coming in waves since the separation, which was about 11 months ago now.\n\nEven before this latest move, I was already having periods where I’d feel really low and weighed down. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It didn’t feel like I was depressed right then. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.\n\nBut it would flip really fast into irritation. Like if something slowed me down even slightly, I’d react as if it was a much bigger deal than it actually was. I snapped at the contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think there can be a low after, or at least I notice this really heavy feeling coming back. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.

And then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.

In the moment it honestly felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way on purpose, even though now I can see that’s not fair.\n\nSo yes, I’d say there have been times when the energetic and irritable feeling was its own thing, not just me being sad before or after.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel a crash or a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there can be a low after, or at least I notice this really heavy feeling coming back. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 34 / web_or_digital_interface_designer / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressed_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling in my chest, and even when the apartment is quiet and nothing bad is happening, I feel like I could cry over a coffee mug in the sink.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared.   },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.

It’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. And the apartment can be completely quiet, nothing bad going on, no emergency, no one saying anything to me, and I’ll see something stupidly small, like a coffee mug left in the sink, and I’ll suddenly feel like I could cry. Not because of the mug exactly, but because in that moment it feels like… I don’t know, like even normal life is too much to keep up with.

So I guess not always in a neat pattern, but yes, I do often seem to come down into that heavy, sad feeling afterward.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Sometimes it feels like maybe there are energetic stretches where I don’t immediately drop afterward, but lately… honestly, I mostly notice myself coming back to that low feeling.

Like I’ll wake up and before I’ve even checked my phone or done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. And it’s strange because nothing has to be wrong — the apartment can be totally quiet, nobody’s bothering me, there’s no actual problem happening — but then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and I feel like I might cry. It’s like that one little thing suddenly stands in for everything I’m not managing.

So I don’t know if I can confidently say, “Yes, I have those energetic periods and then no low afterward.” Maybe once in a while, but what stands out to me lately is that I keep ending up in that sad, heavy place afterward.

It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.\n\nIt’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. Not because of the mug exactly, but because in that moment it feels like… I don’t know, like even normal life is too much to keep up with.\n\nSo I guess not always in a neat pattern, but yes, I do often seem to come down into that heavy, sad feeling afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like maybe there are energetic stretches where I don’t immediately drop afterward, but lately… honestly, I mostly notice myself coming back to that low feeling.\n\nLike I’ll wake up and before I’ve even checked my phone or done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. It’s like that one little thing suddenly stands in for everything I’m not managing.\n\nSo I don’t know if I can confidently say, “Yes, I have those energetic periods and then no low afterward.” Maybe once in a while, but what stands out to me lately is that I keep ending up in that sad, heavy place afterward.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward that lasts for an extended time?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly, honestly. Like, I can’t point to an energetic period and say, “Yes, that one was followed by months of feeling totally fine.” What I can say is that the heavier low stretches have been around for a while now — since the separation, which was about eleven months ago.

And that was before this latest move, too. The move made everything feel sharper, like it brought the heaviness more into focus or made it harder to ignore, but it didn’t start there. It had already been showing up after the separation. So when you ask if I’ve had those energetic periods without a long low afterward… maybe not in a way I can confidently identify, because the low periods have kind of been part of the background for almost a year now, with some stretches feeling worse than others.

I took on extra client prototype work. And then I started completely redesigning my portfolio, like not just updating a few pieces, but acting like I needed to overhaul the whole thing immediately. Like everything needed to be optimized and rebuilt right now, and I couldn’t just leave things alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or energetic during this period of taking on extra work and redesigning your portfolio?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling almost electric, like everything was suddenly possible and I was weirdly upbeat about it. Not just “in a good mood,” more like I had this charge under my skin and I could see all these fixes and improvements everywhere.\n\nBut it wasn’t only happy, exactly. It could switch really fast. One minute I’d be excited, talking too fast about some portfolio idea or how the apartment was finally going to come together, and then if something slowed me down even a little, I’d get so irritated. Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. In the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way of this whole plan I had to get everything moving.\n\nThen afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. It’s hard to describe because it wasn’t just “I had a lot of energy.” It felt almost electric, like I was plugged into something, and I’d be really upbeat and convinced I could handle everything all at once.\n\nBut at the same time, the irritation was right there under it. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I’m not sure I can say yes in a clean way. Maybe there are times where I feel more up or energized and then I don’t immediately crash in some obvious, dramatic way, but lately what I notice is that I keep ending up back in this low, heavy place.

It’s like I’ll wake up and there’s already this gray feeling sitting in my chest before anything has even happened. The apartment can be quiet, nothing bad going on, no message or problem waiting for me, and then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug in the sink, and it feels like I could just start crying. Not really because of the mug, but because it somehow makes everything feel too much.

So… maybe sometimes there isn’t an immediate low afterward, but what stands out to me right now is that the sadness keeps coming back.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess I’m here because things have felt… really out of proportion with me lately. I just moved into a new apartment, and at first I could barely get myself settled — like boxes everywhere, half-unpacked kitchen stuff, that kind of thing — and then all of a sudden I sort of flipped into this mode where I was saying yes to everything.\n\nI agreed to do mentoring sessions, even though I hadn’t really planned for that. I took on extra client prototype work. And then I started completely redesigning my portfolio, like not just updating a few pieces, but acting like I needed to overhaul the whole thing immediately. It felt like I had to fix my entire life in one sprint, if that makes sense. Like everything needed to be optimized and rebuilt right now, and I couldn’t just leave things alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or energetic during this period of taking on extra work and redesigning your portfolio?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling almost electric, like everything was suddenly possible and I was weirdly upbeat about it. Not just “in a good mood,” more like I had this charge under my skin and I could see all these fixes and improvements everywhere.\n\nBut it wasn’t only happy, exactly. It could switch really fast. One minute I’d be excited, talking too fast about some portfolio idea or how the apartment was finally going to come together, and then if something slowed me down even a little, I’d get so irritated. Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. In the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way of this whole plan I had to get everything moving.\n\nThen afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. It’s hard to describe because it wasn’t just “I had a lot of energy.” It felt almost electric, like I was plugged into something, and I’d be really upbeat and convinced I could handle everything all at once.\n\nBut at the same time, the irritation was right there under it. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. It’s been happening lately in a way that feels really physical, not just like I’m having a bad day or I’m stressed.\n\nI’ll wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. They’ve been kind of coming in waves since the separation, which was about 11 months ago now.\n\nEven before this latest move, I was already having periods where I’d feel really low and weighed down. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It didn’t feel like I was depressed right then. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.\n\nBut it would flip really fast into irritation. Like if something slowed me down even slightly, I’d react as if it was a much bigger deal than it actually was. I snapped at the contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too. In the moment it honestly felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way on purpose, even though now I can see that’s not fair.\n\nSo yes, I’d say there have been times when the energetic and irritable feeling was its own thing, not just me being sad before or after.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel a crash or a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there can be a low after, or at least I notice this really heavy feeling coming back. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.\n\nIt’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. And the apartment can be completely quiet, nothing bad going on, no emergency, no one saying anything to me, and I’ll see something stupidly small, like a coffee mug left in the sink, and I’ll suddenly feel like I could cry. Not because of the mug exactly, but because in that moment it feels like… I don’t know, like even normal life is too much to keep up with.\n\nSo I guess not always in a neat pattern, but yes, I do often seem to come down into that heavy, sad feeling afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like maybe there are energetic stretches where I don’t immediately drop afterward, but lately… honestly, I mostly notice myself coming back to that low feeling.\n\nLike I’ll wake up and before I’ve even checked my phone or done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. And it’s strange because nothing has to be wrong — the apartment can be totally quiet, nobody’s bothering me, there’s no actual problem happening — but then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and I feel like I might cry. It’s like that one little thing suddenly stands in for everything I’m not managing.\n\nSo I don’t know if I can confidently say, “Yes, I have those energetic periods and then no low afterward.” Maybe once in a while, but what stands out to me lately is that I keep ending up in that sad, heavy place afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward that lasts for an extended time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly, honestly. Like, I can’t point to an energetic period and say, “Yes, that one was followed by months of feeling totally fine.” What I can say is that the heavier low stretches have been around for a while now — since the separation, which was about eleven months ago.\n\nAnd that was before this latest move, too. The move made everything feel sharper, like it brought the heaviness more into focus or made it harder to ignore, but it didn’t start there. It had already been showing up after the separation. So when you ask if I’ve had those energetic periods without a long low afterward… maybe not in a way I can confidently identify, because the low periods have kind of been part of the background for almost a year now, with some stretches feeling worse than others.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not sure I can say yes in a clean way. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. They’ve been kind of coming in waves since the separation, which was about 11 months ago now.\n\nEven before this latest move, I was already having periods where I’d feel really low and weighed down. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It didn’t feel like I was depressed right then. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.\n\nBut it would flip really fast into irritation. Like if something slowed me down even slightly, I’d react as if it was a much bigger deal than it actually was. I snapped at the contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too. In the moment it honestly felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way on purpose, even though now I can see that’s not fair.\n\nSo yes, I’d say there have been times when the energetic and irritable feeling was its own thing, not just me being sad before or after.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel a crash or a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there can be a low after, or at least I notice this really heavy feeling coming back. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.\n\nIt’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. And the apartment can be completely quiet, nothing bad going on, no emergency, no one saying anything to me, and I’ll see something stupidly small, like a coffee mug left in the sink, and I’ll suddenly feel like I could cry. Not because of the mug exactly, but because in that moment it feels like… I don’t know, like even normal life is too much to keep up with.\n\nSo I guess not always in a neat pattern, but yes, I do often seem to come down into that heavy, sad feeling afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like maybe there are energetic stretches where I don’t immediately drop afterward, but lately… honestly, I mostly notice myself coming back to that low feeling.\n\nLike I’ll wake up and before I’ve even checked my phone or done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. And it’s strange because nothing has to be wrong — the apartment can be totally quiet, nobody’s bothering me, there’s no actual problem happening — but then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and I feel like I might cry. It’s like that one little thing suddenly stands in for everything I’m not managing.\n\nSo I don’t know if I can confidently say, “Yes, I have those energetic periods and then no low afterward.” Maybe once in a while, but what stands out to me lately is that I keep ending up in that sad, heavy place afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward that lasts for an extended time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly, honestly. Like, I can’t point to an energetic period and say, “Yes, that one was followed by months of feeling totally fine.” What I can say is that the heavier low stretches have been around for a while now — since the separation, which was about eleven months ago.\n\nAnd that was before this latest move, too. The move made everything feel sharper, like it brought the heaviness more into focus or made it harder to ignore, but it didn’t start there. It had already been showing up after the separation. Not always in a neat, immediate way, no. I don’t know if every energetic stretch has this obvious “and then I crash” pattern right after it. But lately, what I keep noticing is that the low feeling is just… there, or it comes back really easily.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say always, because it’s not like I can map it perfectly — like, energetic period, then low period, every single time in a clean sequence. It feels messier than that.

But lately, the low feeling is what I keep noticing most. I’ll wake up and there’s already this heavy, gray feeling in my chest before anything has even happened. The apartment can be completely quiet, no crisis, no bad message on my phone, nothing really wrong in that moment, and I still feel like I’m right on the edge of crying.

And then it can be something so stupidly small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly it feels overwhelming. Not because the mug matters, obviously, but because it’s like this tiny thing somehow confirms that everything is too much. So… maybe I don’t always end up low in a neat way after being energized, but I do keep ending up back in that low place.

Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.\n\nIt’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think I’ve kind of already said this, but… no, I don’t know that I can say it always happens in a clear, predictable way after those energetic periods. I wish I could give you a cleaner answer than that, like yes or no, but it doesn’t feel that organized from the inside.

What I can say is that lately I keep waking up with this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like before anything has even happened, before I’ve checked my phone or talked to anyone or had some obvious reason to feel bad, it’s already there. The apartment can be totally quiet, nothing going wrong, and I’ll see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and somehow it feels like I could just start crying over it.

And I know that sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug. But in the moment it doesn’t feel small. It feels like this little thing that somehow proves I’m overwhelmed or not okay. So I wouldn’t say I always end up low right after being energetic, exactly, but I do keep finding myself back in that low place.

Like everything needed to be optimized and rebuilt right now, and I couldn’t just leave things alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or energetic during this period of taking on extra work and redesigning your portfolio?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling almost electric, like everything was suddenly possible and I was weirdly upbeat about it. Not just “in a good mood,” more like I had this charge under my skin and I could see all these fixes and improvements everywhere.\n\nBut it wasn’t only happy, exactly. It could switch really fast. One minute I’d be excited, talking too fast about some portfolio idea or how the apartment was finally going to come together, and then if something slowed me down even a little, I’d get so irritated. Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. In the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way of this whole plan I had to get everything moving.\n\nThen afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. It’s hard to describe because it wasn’t just “I had a lot of energy.” It felt almost electric, like I was plugged into something, and I’d be really upbeat and convinced I could handle everything all at once.\n\nBut at the same time, the irritation was right there under it. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. It’s been happening lately in a way that feels really physical, not just like I’m having a bad day or I’m stressed.\n\nI’ll wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.\n\nBut it would flip really fast into irritation. I’m sorry, I know I’m sort of repeating myself, but I don’t think I can honestly say it always happens right after, like there’s a perfect cause-and-effect pattern. It’s more that lately I keep finding myself in that low place again.

Like everything needed to be optimized and rebuilt right now, and I couldn’t just leave things alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or energetic during this period of taking on extra work and redesigning your portfolio?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling almost electric, like everything was suddenly possible and I was weirdly upbeat about it. Not just “in a good mood,” more like I had this charge under my skin and I could see all these fixes and improvements everywhere.\n\nBut it wasn’t only happy, exactly. It could switch really fast. One minute I’d be excited, talking too fast about some portfolio idea or how the apartment was finally going to come together, and then if something slowed me down even a little, I’d get so irritated. Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. In the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way of this whole plan I had to get everything moving.\n\nThen afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. It’s hard to describe because it wasn’t just “I had a lot of energy.” It felt almost electric, like I was plugged into something, and I’d be really upbeat and convinced I could handle everything all at once.\n\nBut at the same time, the irritation was right there under it. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. It’s been happening lately in a way that feels really physical, not just like I’m having a bad day or I’m stressed.\n\nI’ll wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. They’ve been kind of coming in waves since the separation, which was about 11 months ago now.\n\nEven before this latest move, I was already having periods where I’d feel really low and weighed down. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It didn’t feel like I was depressed right then. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.\n\nBut it would flip really fast into irritation. Like if something slowed me down even slightly, I’d react as if it was a much bigger deal than it actually was. I snapped at the contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too. In the moment it honestly felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way on purpose, even though now I can see that’s not fair.\n\nSo yes, I’d say there have been times when the energetic and irritable feeling was its own thing, not just me being sad before or after.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel a crash or a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there can be a low after, or at least I notice this really heavy feeling coming back. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.\n\nIt’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. And the apartment can be completely quiet, nothing bad going on, no emergency, no one saying anything to me, and I’ll see something stupidly small, like a coffee mug left in the sink, and I’ll suddenly feel like I could cry. Not because of the mug exactly, but because in that moment it feels like… I don’t know, like even normal life is too much to keep up with.\n\nSo I guess not always in a neat pattern, but yes, I do often seem to come down into that heavy, sad feeling afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like maybe there are energetic stretches where I don’t immediately drop afterward, but lately… honestly, I mostly notice myself coming back to that low feeling.\n\nLike I’ll wake up and before I’ve even checked my phone or done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. And it’s strange because nothing has to be wrong — the apartment can be totally quiet, nobody’s bothering me, there’s no actual problem happening — but then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and I feel like I might cry. It’s like that one little thing suddenly stands in for everything I’m not managing.\n\nSo I don’t know if I can confidently say, “Yes, I have those energetic periods and then no low afterward.” Maybe once in a while, but what stands out to me lately is that I keep ending up in that sad, heavy place afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward that lasts for an extended time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly, honestly. Like, I can’t point to an energetic period and say, “Yes, that one was followed by months of feeling totally fine.” What I can say is that the heavier low stretches have been around for a while now — since the separation, which was about eleven months ago.\n\nAnd that was before this latest move, too. The move made everything feel sharper, like it brought the heaviness more into focus or made it harder to ignore, but it didn’t start there. It had already been showing up after the separation. So when you ask if I’ve had those energetic periods without a long low afterward… maybe not in a way I can confidently identify, because the low periods have kind of been part of the background for almost a year now, with some stretches feeling worse than others.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not sure I can say yes in a clean way. Maybe there are times where I feel more up or energized and then I don’t immediately crash in some obvious, dramatic way, but lately what I notice is that I keep ending up back in this low, heavy place.\n\nIt’s like I’ll wake up and there’s already this gray feeling sitting in my chest before anything has even happened. The apartment can be quiet, nothing bad going on, no message or problem waiting for me, and then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug in the sink, and it feels like I could just start crying. Not really because of the mug, but because it somehow makes everything feel too much.\n\nSo… maybe sometimes there isn’t an immediate low afterward, but what stands out to me right now is that the sadness keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Not always in a neat, immediate way, no. I don’t know if every energetic stretch has this obvious “and then I crash” pattern right after it. But lately, what I keep noticing is that the low feeling is just… there, or it comes back really easily.\n\nI’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, I already have this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day hasn’t started, the apartment is quiet, there’s no bad news, nobody’s asking anything of me, and somehow I already feel like I’m on the edge of crying. And then it can be something completely small, like seeing a coffee mug left in the sink, and it just hits me way harder than it should. It’s not really about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that I’m not keeping up with anything.\n\nSo I guess I wouldn’t say “always” after the energetic periods, because I’m not sure. But the low mood has been showing up enough lately that it feels like I keep landing back there.\n\nDoctor: Do you always end up feeling low after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, because it’s not like I can map it perfectly — like, energetic period, then low period, every single time in a clean sequence. It feels messier than that.\n\nBut lately, the low feeling is what I keep noticing most. I’ll wake up and there’s already this heavy, gray feeling in my chest before anything has even happened. The apartment can be completely quiet, no crisis, no bad message on my phone, nothing really wrong in that moment, and I still feel like I’m right on the edge of crying.\n\nAnd then it can be something so stupidly small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly it feels overwhelming. Not because the mug matters, obviously, but because it’s like this tiny thing somehow confirms that everything is too much. So… maybe I don’t always end up low in a neat way after being energized, but I do keep ending up back in that low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always end up feeling low after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I think I’ve kind of already said this, but… no, I don’t know that I can say it always happens in a clear, predictable way after those energetic periods. I wish I could give you a cleaner answer than that, like yes or no, but it doesn’t feel that organized from the inside.\n\nWhat I can say is that lately I keep waking up with this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like before anything has even happened, before I’ve checked my phone or talked to anyone or had some obvious reason to feel bad, it’s already there. The apartment can be totally quiet, nothing going wrong, and I’ll see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and somehow it feels like I could just start crying over it.\n\nAnd I know that sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug. But in the moment it doesn’t feel small. It feels like this little thing that somehow proves I’m overwhelmed or not okay. So I wouldn’t say I always end up low right after being energetic, exactly, but I do keep finding myself back in that low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always end up feeling low after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m sort of repeating myself, but I don’t think I can honestly say it always happens right after, like there’s a perfect cause-and-effect pattern. It’s more that lately I keep finding myself in that low place again.\n\nI’ll wake up and it’s already there, this heavy, gray feeling in my chest, before anything has even happened. The apartment can be completely quiet, no bad news, no argument, nothing actually wrong, and I still feel like I’m about to cry. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I know I’ve kind of answered this already, but… no, I don’t think I can say it always happens in that neat order, like I’m energetic and then immediately I crash. It doesn’t feel that tidy from the inside.

What I notice more is that lately I keep waking up already low. Like there’s this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest before the day has even started. Nothing has to happen. The apartment can be completely quiet, there’s no bad news, no argument, nothing obvious, and I still feel like I’m right on the edge of crying.

And then something tiny, like seeing a coffee mug in the sink, can just set off this feeling of… I don’t know, like everything is too much. I know it’s just a mug, and part of me knows that’s not rational, but emotionally it feels huge in that moment. So I wouldn’t say always after those energetic periods, exactly, but I do keep ending up back in that really low place.

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I know I’ve kind of said this already, but I don’t know that I’d call it “always,” like it follows some perfect sequence every single time. It’s not that clean in my head.

What I can say is that lately I keep waking up already feeling low, before anything has even happened. It’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest, like the day starts with something sitting on me. And the apartment can be quiet, nothing bad going on, no obvious reason for me to be upset, and I’ll still feel like I could cry.

Sometimes it’s almost embarrassing how small the thing is that brings it up. Like I’ll see a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly it feels overwhelming, like this tiny proof that I’m not managing. I know, logically, it’s just a mug. But in that moment it feels much bigger than that. So… not always right after an energetic period, no, but I do keep coming back to that low feeling.

It felt like I had to fix my entire life in one sprint, if that makes sense. Like everything needed to be optimized and rebuilt right now, and I couldn’t just leave things alone.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or energetic during this period of taking on extra work and redesigning your portfolio?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling almost electric, like everything was suddenly possible and I was weirdly upbeat about it. Not just “in a good mood,” more like I had this charge under my skin and I could see all these fixes and improvements everywhere.\n\nBut it wasn’t only happy, exactly. It could switch really fast. One minute I’d be excited, talking too fast about some portfolio idea or how the apartment was finally going to come together, and then if something slowed me down even a little, I’d get so irritated. Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. In the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way of this whole plan I had to get everything moving.\n\nThen afterward I’d think, why did I react like that? It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. It’s been happening lately in a way that feels really physical, not just like I’m having a bad day or I’m stressed.\n\nI’ll wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. They’ve been kind of coming in waves since the separation, which was about 11 months ago now.\n\nEven before this latest move, I was already having periods where I’d feel really low and weighed down. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It didn’t feel like I was depressed right then. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.\n\nBut it would flip really fast into irritation. Like if something slowed me down even slightly, I’d react as if it was a much bigger deal than it actually was. I snapped at the contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too. In the moment it honestly felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way on purpose, even though now I can see that’s not fair.\n\nSo yes, I’d say there have been times when the energetic and irritable feeling was its own thing, not just me being sad before or after.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel a crash or a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there can be a low after, or at least I notice this really heavy feeling coming back. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.\n\nIt’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. And the apartment can be completely quiet, nothing bad going on, no emergency, no one saying anything to me, and I’ll see something stupidly small, like a coffee mug left in the sink, and I’ll suddenly feel like I could cry. Not because of the mug exactly, but because in that moment it feels like… I don’t know, like even normal life is too much to keep up with.\n\nSo I guess not always in a neat pattern, but yes, I do often seem to come down into that heavy, sad feeling afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like maybe there are energetic stretches where I don’t immediately drop afterward, but lately… honestly, I mostly notice myself coming back to that low feeling.\n\nLike I’ll wake up and before I’ve even checked my phone or done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. And it’s strange because nothing has to be wrong — the apartment can be totally quiet, nobody’s bothering me, there’s no actual problem happening — but then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and I feel like I might cry. It’s like that one little thing suddenly stands in for everything I’m not managing.\n\nSo I don’t know if I can confidently say, “Yes, I have those energetic periods and then no low afterward.” Maybe once in a while, but what stands out to me lately is that I keep ending up in that sad, heavy place afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward that lasts for an extended time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly, honestly. Like, I can’t point to an energetic period and say, “Yes, that one was followed by months of feeling totally fine.” What I can say is that the heavier low stretches have been around for a while now — since the separation, which was about eleven months ago.\n\nAnd that was before this latest move, too. The move made everything feel sharper, like it brought the heaviness more into focus or made it harder to ignore, but it didn’t start there. It had already been showing up after the separation. So when you ask if I’ve had those energetic periods without a long low afterward… maybe not in a way I can confidently identify, because the low periods have kind of been part of the background for almost a year now, with some stretches feeling worse than others.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not sure I can say yes in a clean way. Maybe there are times where I feel more up or energized and then I don’t immediately crash in some obvious, dramatic way, but lately what I notice is that I keep ending up back in this low, heavy place.\n\nIt’s like I’ll wake up and there’s already this gray feeling sitting in my chest before anything has even happened. The apartment can be quiet, nothing bad going on, no message or problem waiting for me, and then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug in the sink, and it feels like I could just start crying. Not really because of the mug, but because it somehow makes everything feel too much.\n\nSo… maybe sometimes there isn’t an immediate low afterward, but what stands out to me right now is that the sadness keeps coming back.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Not always in a neat, immediate way, no. I don’t know if every energetic stretch has this obvious “and then I crash” pattern right after it. But lately, what I keep noticing is that the low feeling is just… there, or it comes back really easily.\n\nI’ll wake up and before anything has even happened, I already have this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day hasn’t started, the apartment is quiet, there’s no bad news, nobody’s asking anything of me, and somehow I already feel like I’m on the edge of crying. And then it can be something completely small, like seeing a coffee mug left in the sink, and it just hits me way harder than it should. It’s not really about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that I’m not keeping up with anything.\n\nSo I guess I wouldn’t say “always” after the energetic periods, because I’m not sure. But the low mood has been showing up enough lately that it feels like I keep landing back there.\n\nDoctor: Do you always end up feeling low after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, because it’s not like I can map it perfectly — like, energetic period, then low period, every single time in a clean sequence. It feels messier than that.\n\nBut lately, the low feeling is what I keep noticing most. I’ll wake up and there’s already this heavy, gray feeling in my chest before anything has even happened. The apartment can be completely quiet, no crisis, no bad message on my phone, nothing really wrong in that moment, and I still feel like I’m right on the edge of crying.\n\nAnd then it can be something so stupidly small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly it feels overwhelming. Not because the mug matters, obviously, but because it’s like this tiny thing somehow confirms that everything is too much. So… maybe I don’t always end up low in a neat way after being energized, but I do keep ending up back in that low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always end up feeling low after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I think I’ve kind of already said this, but… no, I don’t know that I can say it always happens in a clear, predictable way after those energetic periods. I wish I could give you a cleaner answer than that, like yes or no, but it doesn’t feel that organized from the inside.\n\nWhat I can say is that lately I keep waking up with this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like before anything has even happened, before I’ve checked my phone or talked to anyone or had some obvious reason to feel bad, it’s already there. The apartment can be totally quiet, nothing going wrong, and I’ll see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and somehow it feels like I could just start crying over it.\n\nAnd I know that sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug. But in the moment it doesn’t feel small. It feels like this little thing that somehow proves I’m overwhelmed or not okay. So I wouldn’t say I always end up low right after being energetic, exactly, but I do keep finding myself back in that low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always end up feeling low after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m sort of repeating myself, but I don’t think I can honestly say it always happens right after, like there’s a perfect cause-and-effect pattern. I feel like I’m repeating myself, but… no, I don’t think it’s always in that exact order. I can’t honestly say every energetic stretch ends and then I immediately drop. It’s messier than that.

Like I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I also got sharp with my sister about something that probably wasn’t a big deal at all. It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. It’s hard to describe because it wasn’t just “I had a lot of energy.” It felt almost electric, like I was plugged into something, and I’d be really upbeat and convinced I could handle everything all at once.\n\nBut at the same time, the irritation was right there under it. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. It’s been happening lately in a way that feels really physical, not just like I’m having a bad day or I’m stressed.\n\nI’ll wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink.
