Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. They’ve been kind of coming in waves since the separation, which was about 11 months ago now.\n\nEven before this latest move, I was already having periods where I’d feel really low and weighed down. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that. It didn’t feel like I was depressed right then. It was more like I was almost buzzing — like electric, overly upbeat, like I had this surge of energy and everything suddenly seemed urgent or possible.\n\nBut it would flip really fast into irritation. Like if something slowed me down even slightly, I’d react as if it was a much bigger deal than it actually was. I snapped at the contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too. In the moment it honestly felt like they were personally blocking me, like they were getting in the way on purpose, even though now I can see that’s not fair.\n\nSo yes, I’d say there have been times when the energetic and irritable feeling was its own thing, not just me being sad before or after.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel a crash or a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there can be a low after, or at least I notice this really heavy feeling coming back. It’s not always like a dramatic “crash” where one day I’m up and the next day I’m completely on the floor, but lately I’ll wake up and it’s like my chest already has this gray weight in it.\n\nAnd then even if nothing is actually happening — like the apartment is quiet, no crisis, no argument, nothing urgent — I’ll just see something small, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and suddenly I feel like I could cry. It sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud, because it’s just a mug, but in that moment it feels like proof that everything is too much, or that I’m not keeping up with even basic things. So yes, after those energized periods, I do seem to end up back in that heavy, low place.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel a low period after one of these extremely energetic periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, like every single time in a perfectly predictable way… but I do know that lately I end up back in that low place a lot.\n\nIt’s this very specific feeling when I wake up — like before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, gray feeling sitting in my chest. And the apartment can be completely quiet, nothing bad going on, no emergency, no one saying anything to me, and I’ll see something stupidly small, like a coffee mug left in the sink, and I’ll suddenly feel like I could cry. Not because of the mug exactly, but because in that moment it feels like… I don’t know, like even normal life is too much to keep up with.\n\nSo I guess not always in a neat pattern, but yes, I do often seem to come down into that heavy, sad feeling afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels like maybe there are energetic stretches where I don’t immediately drop afterward, but lately… honestly, I mostly notice myself coming back to that low feeling.\n\nLike I’ll wake up and before I’ve even checked my phone or done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. And it’s strange because nothing has to be wrong — the apartment can be totally quiet, nobody’s bothering me, there’s no actual problem happening — but then I’ll see something tiny, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink, and I feel like I might cry. It’s like that one little thing suddenly stands in for everything I’m not managing.\n\nSo I don’t know if I can confidently say, “Yes, I have those energetic periods and then no low afterward.” Maybe once in a while, but what stands out to me lately is that I keep ending up in that sad, heavy place afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward that lasts for an extended time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly, honestly. Like, I can’t point to an energetic period and say, “Yes, that one was followed by months of feeling totally fine.” What I can say is that the heavier low stretches have been around for a while now — since the separation, which was about eleven months ago.\n\nAnd that was before this latest move, too. The move made everything feel sharper, like it brought the heaviness more into focus or made it harder to ignore, but it didn’t start there. It had already been showing up after the separation. So when you ask if I’ve had those energetic periods without a long low afterward… maybe not in a way I can confidently identify, because the low periods have kind of been part of the background for almost a year now, with some stretches feeling worse than others.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have one of these extremely energetic periods without feeling a low period afterward?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not sure I can say yes in a clean way. Yes, I think so. I mean, they don’t feel like just “me having a good day” or being a little more productive than usual. They feel like I’ve shifted into this different gear, and it’s hard to explain without making it sound dramatic.

Earlier this month, there were moments where I felt almost electric, like everything in me was turned up. I’d be really upbeat and sure of myself, like I could push through anything, and then if something got in the way even a little bit, I’d get irritated so fast. Like a contractor was delayed with something, or my sister didn’t respond or move as quickly as I thought she should, and I snapped at them like they were personally trying to block me.

And looking back, the reaction didn’t really match the situation. It wasn’t like they’d done something terrible. It was more like any tiny delay felt intolerable in that state. So yes, I’d say those periods feel separate from my normal mood, because normally I don’t feel that wired-up and reactive over such small things.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I do. I mean, I know I kind of answered this already, but it really does feel separate from my normal state. It’s not just that I’m in a better mood or I have more energy to get things done. It feels like I’m suddenly… I don’t know, almost electric.

Earlier this month especially, I remember feeling really upbeat and charged, like everything was moving quickly in my head and I was very sure of myself. But then the mood could flip into irritation so fast. Like if a contractor was delayed or didn’t respond the way I expected, or if my sister took too long with something, I’d snap at them. And in the moment it felt like they were personally blocking me or getting in my way on purpose.

Then afterward I’d look back and think, why was I that intense about such a small delay? So yes, I’d say those periods feel distinct from how I usually am.

It was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt extremely energetic and irritable at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though the most obvious one was earlier this month. It’s hard to describe because it wasn’t just “I had a lot of energy.” It felt almost electric, like I was plugged into something, and I’d be really upbeat and convinced I could handle everything all at once.\n\nBut at the same time, the irritation was right there under it. Like if anything slowed me down even a little, it felt intolerable. I snapped at a contractor over a tiny delay, and I got short with my sister too, over something that probably didn’t deserve that level of reaction. In my head, in the moment, it felt like they were personally blocking me or interfering with what I needed to get done, even though looking back I can see that was kind of extreme.\n\nSo yes, the energetic and irritable parts were happening together. It wasn’t calm productivity — it was more like being revved up and easily set off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling extremely down or depressed in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I have. It’s been happening lately in a way that feels really physical, not just like I’m having a bad day or I’m stressed.\n\nI’ll wake up and before I’ve even really done anything, there’s this heavy, gray feeling in my chest. Like the day already has weight to it. And sometimes the apartment will be completely quiet, nothing bad is actively happening, and I’ll just look around and feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug sitting in the sink. It’s not even about the mug, obviously, but in the moment it feels like this tiny proof that everything is too much or that I’m failing at basic life somehow.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say because from the outside it probably looks so minor — just normal apartment stuff, normal mess — but emotionally it can hit me really hard. Like I’m already carrying something when I wake up, and then some tiny ordinary thing makes it spill over.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling extremely down or depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It varies, but the heavier stretches… those haven’t just been a day or two. They’ve been kind of coming in waves since the separation, which was about 11 months ago now.\n\nEven before this latest move, I was already having periods where I’d feel really low and weighed down. The move just made it sharper, I think — like everything got more concentrated and harder to ignore. But it didn’t start with the move. Looking back, the really heavy depressed times have been showing up on and off since the separation, and sometimes it feels like I only notice how long it’s been when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced one of these extremely energetic and irritable periods without being depressed either right before or after it?\n\nPatient: I think so, yes… at least earlier this month it felt like that.
